市場焦點
美國
• 能源價格上漲帶動 5月CPI與PPI同步走高，短期通膨壓力有所增加 ，但核心通膨及中長期通膨
預期仍維持相對穩定，顯示全面性物價壓力尚未明顯擴大 ，尤其是就業市場穩健與經濟具支撐
力下，聯準會短期內降息空間有限 ，高利率環境可能延續。投資上可採取「抗通膨＋成長」雙
主軸策略，配置能源與基礎建設資產 ，同時布局AI科技龍頭及高評級投資等級債 ，以兼顧成長
與收益機會。
AI
供應鏈
• 彭博預估，2026年MSCI世界指數中的AI股票，平均獲利將攀升61%，這一增速幾乎是該指數
其餘成分股預期增速 （15%）的四倍 。分析顯示 ，雲服務巨頭的資本支出在 2025年已突破
4,000億美元，五年內成長超過兩倍 ，預計至2027年將上看1兆美元，象徵雲端與AI正進入長

期結構性成長階段，在基本面展望與資金面支撐下，全球AI供應鏈有望維持偏多格局。
新興市場債
• 對本幣債券採取減碼與汰弱留強策略 ，調降新興市場本幣債券的整體配置 ，若要保留本幣債，
應將資金集中在遠期市場預期殖利率仍有望下探、貨幣相對具備韌性的少數例外國家。
• 美元信用債落袋為安或轉向防禦 ，提防利差反彈風險，未來利差再收窄的空間極為有限 ，反而
面臨金融環境一旦轉緊、利差大幅彈升的下行風險。

能源成本推升物價，利率前景預期面臨修正
• 能源價格帶動CPI續揚，通膨降溫進程出現干擾： 美國5
月CPI年增率升至4.2%，創近三年最大漲幅 ，月增0.5%，
主要受到能源價格單月上漲3.9%帶動，其中汽油價格年
增40.5% 為 主 要 推 升 因 素 ； 相 較 之 下， 核心 CPI 月增
0.2%、年增2.9%，顯示本波通膨上升仍以能源因素為
主，內需帶動的價格壓力尚未明顯擴大 ，但若能源價格
持續高檔，將逐步反映至其他商品與服務價格。
• PPI漲幅擴大，企業成本壓力升溫： 5月PPI月增1.1%、
年增6.5%，創2022年底以來最大增幅 ，能源相關商品
價格上漲推升企業生產成本 ，核心PPI亦同步走高 ，顯
示部分成本壓力已逐漸擴散至其他產業 。由於PPI通常

領先反映終端售價變化 ，若能源價格未明顯回落 ，未來
數月核心通膨及PCE仍可能面臨上行風險。
• 後市觀察： 能源價格仍是短期通膨走向的重要變數 ，若
油價逐步回穩 ，通膨壓力可望緩解；在核心通膨尚未明
顯升溫下，聯準會短期內重啟升息機率有限 ，但降息時
程可能延後 ，建議投資人可適度配置能源 、天然資源及
全球基礎建設相關資產，作為抗通膨與分散風險工具。
美國 CPI 與核心 CPI 趨勢比較
資料來源：Bloomberg，U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，2026.6.10

高利率環境預期延續，消費信心與財務轉趨保守
• 通膨預期維持穩定，緊縮壓力暫緩：紐約聯準銀行5月消費者預期調查顯示，未來1年通膨預期由3.6%降至3.5%，3年與5
年期通膨預期則分別維持於 3.1%與3.0%，顯示近期能源價格與地緣政治因素雖帶來短期干擾 ，但市場對中長期通膨走勢
看法仍相對穩定，反映聯準會2022年以來持續推動縮表等緊縮措施對控制通膨預期仍具成效。
• 家庭支出態度趨保守，降息預期續延後：調查同時顯示，美國家庭對未來財務狀況與就業前景態度趨於審慎，高利率環境
已逐步影響消費與支出規劃，加上就業市場仍維持韌性，使市場對聯準會提前降息的期待進一步降溫，利率可能在高檔維
持更長時間。
• 投資建議：在通膨預期穩定、經濟尚未顯著轉弱的情境下，降息預期持續後延。投資策略建議聚焦AI基礎建設、數位轉型

受惠企業及具穩定現金流的大型優質企業，同時搭配投資等級債券，以提升投組收益並強化防禦能力。
資料來源：New York Fed Survey of Consumer Expectations， 2026.6.9

歐洲央行宣布升息一碼
• 歐洲央行宣布升息一碼：主要再融資利率、存款便利利率及邊際貸款利率將分別調升一碼至 2.40%、2.25% 及 2.65%。經
濟數據預測方面，受到中東衝突影響，上調通膨預測至3%(先前2.6%)、下調GDP預估至0.8%(先前0.9%)。理事會強調，此
次升息旨在確保通膨在中期內能穩定在2%的目標水準，且此決策在多種衝突演變的情境下均具備穩健性。
• 歐股表現回穩：歐洲股市(歐洲600指數)在連續三個月落後美國股市後，6月以來扭轉頹勢。市場預期伊朗戰爭即將結束，加
上對人工智慧炒作的擔憂日益加劇，使得歐洲價值股再次成為多元分散策略的投資選擇。
• 觀點：儘管歐洲央行下修經濟成長預測，但主因來自本次中東衝突造成的「事件性」能源通膨，隨著美伊初步已達成和平協

議，歐股短期估值修正的壓力緩解，中長期來看經濟穩健成長，有望位歐股帶來慢牛行情。
資料來源：歐洲央行、Bloomberg，2026.6.11。

中國外貿穩健成長，AI需求抵消了能源衝擊
• 中國外貿穩健成長 ，AI需求抵消了能源衝擊： 中國5月出口數據增速
由4月的14.1%升至19.4%，連續第二個月加快，優於市場預期，反映
全球科技周期回升 ，外需支撐已有效抵銷地緣政治與能源衝擊的不利
影響。其中高科技產品為主要驅動力 ，出口占比已接近三成 ，自去年
11月以來維持加速態勢 ，5月同比增幅達51%，顯示AI(如伺服器、晶
片與相關設備)需求持續擴張，此外，電動車出口維持強勁 ，5月汽車
出貨量同比增長39%，凸顯新能源產業，在能源價格波動背景下 ，反
而受惠的特性。
• 出口國家方面：對美出口在低基期下大幅反彈 ，對非美市場亦同步擴
張，韓國、日本及東協出口明顯加速 ，反映亞洲供應鏈內部需求強勁 ，
然而，對歐洲(含歐盟及英國 )出口增速明顯放緩 ，或反映當地需求疲

弱與政策不確定性。
• 投資建議： 中國5月出口數據 ，緩解市場對中東局勢拖累中國出口的
疑慮，同時進一步驗證，全球科技需求正逐步取代傳統需求 ，成為貿
易主導力量，對投資而言，建議持續關注中國AI硬體供應鏈與新能源
汽車產業鏈相關標的 ，惟6月聯準會利率決議之前 ，市場將在反覆的
通膨預期中震盪，可透過定期定額佈局分散風險 。
低基期下提振中國對美出口，
對非美市場出口成長也有所加快
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.6.9

AI將成為中國中長期生產力提升的核心動能
• 中美AI產業對其經濟增長貢獻存在差異： 當前美國AI對GDP拉動約20%左右而中國不足10%，此差距主要源於發展戰略不
同。美國採取「算力擴張優先」模式，大規模投入AI基礎設施建設，中國則採取「算力效率優先」路徑，更注重降低訓練
成本與提升應用效率 ，強調AI與實體經濟的深度融合 ，在投資節奏上相對審慎 ，因此短期內對GDP的直接拉動效應較弱 ，
隨著國產替代在成熟製程 、儲存及先進封裝逐步突破 ，未來AI產業鏈自主性提升 ，貿易逆差有望收窄 ，中長期對GDP的貢
獻亦將逐步增强。
• 展望後市：摩根士丹利指出，AI將成為中國中長期生產力提升的核心動能 ，預計至2035年中國GDP增速有望較無AI情境提
升約3.5個百分點，高盛亦指出預計至 2030年，中國採用AI實現自動化的企業比例將提升至 30%，帶動整體經濟動能上揚 ，

預計每年可為中國GDP增長貢獻約0.2至0.3個百分點，中國AI產業後市看好可中長期布局。
資料來源：東吳證券，2026.6.3
中國GPU國產化率持續提升（%）
資料來源：摩根士丹利，2026.6

新興市場債券-本幣債券的隱憂：外匯風險已悄然凌駕利率風險
• 偏鷹派央行與遠期市場的預警：在美伊戰爭爆發後，能源與大宗商品價格走高推升全球通膨預期 。為了對抗潛在的滯脹風險，
各國央行紛紛轉向偏鷹派的立場 ，這徹底打破了市場原本預期聯儲會或全球央行會在年底前降息的幻想 。從遠期市場定價來
看，在17個符合指數條件的新興市場中 ，高達15個本幣債券殖利率預期將繼續走高 ，僅匈牙利和印尼例外 ，這意味著新興
市場本幣債券的價格未來恐面臨下跌。
• 存續期間與外匯的雙重夾擊，亞洲衝擊尤甚：今年以來，新興市場本幣債券表現看起來很有韌性 ，主要是因為過去累積持有
收益(即債券票息)提供288個基點緩衝，掩蓋債券本身跌幅。然而，殖利率上升導致存續期間(duration)拖累192個基點，其中

韓國、泰國、印尼和菲律賓等亞洲市場因為前期貨幣寬鬆與降息 ，在此次債市拋售中承受較大損失 。當前全球正面臨停滯性
通膨威脅，19個新興市場貨幣中有 10個已經走弱，尤其是亞洲石油進口國 (如印度、韓國、印尼、泰國、菲律賓)貨幣跌幅佔
整體外匯負面拖累的83%。隨著時間推移，外匯走弱帶來的匯兌損失，其風險正開始超越單純的利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.6.9

新興市場債券-美元信用債的防禦：利差收窄與美債實際殖利率的拉鋸
• 寬鬆金融環境促使信用利差降至歷史低點： 相較於本幣債券同時承受匯率與利率雙重壓力 ，新興市場美元信用債今年以來逆
勢上漲1.6%，這主要歸功於兩大因素(1)利差收窄：美國整體金融狀況極度寬鬆 (彭博美國金融狀況指數飆升至 1.14，處於歷
史前2%寬鬆區間)，帶動新興市場美元債信用利差大幅收窄 90個基點，目前的平均期權調整利差 (OAS)降至166個基點，處
於2000年指數設立以來的歷史底部 3.5%區間，這代表市場幾乎完全消化戰爭所帶來的風險溢價 。(2)票息收益：今年以來提
供245個基點的穩定正回報，成功對沖美債殖利率上升衝擊。
• 美國政府債務高企，實際殖利率推升債市風險： 今年的美債拋售，除了戰爭引發的通膨預期外，更深層原因在於實質殖利率

的飆升。由於美國政府未償債務總額已達到 30.7萬億美元，逼近美國GDP的100%，在債務負擔沉重 、價格壓力背景下 ，聯
準會實施貨幣寬鬆的空間嚴重受限 。彭博行業研究的模型預估 ，未來12個月美債各天期殖利率仍有7到32個基點的上升空間，
這意味著美元債的存續期間(duration)很難再像過去一樣帶來大幅的資本利得。
• 總結及建議：綜合新興市場本幣債與美元信用債的狀況來看 ，新興市場債券表面上看似穩健 ，背後其實隱藏著本幣債的外匯
貶值危機，以及美元債信用利差已收窄至極致 、缺乏安全邊際的現實。隨著票息防禦緩衝力降至多年低點 ，投資人不能再盲
目地押注全球央行降息。投資建議如下：
✓ 對本幣債券採取減碼與汰弱留強策略：調降新興市場本幣債券的整體配置，特別是亞洲依賴石油進口的國家(如韓國、泰國、
印尼、菲律賓)，因為這些國家正處於通膨與經濟放緩的雙重匯率利空交叉點。若要保留本幣債，應將資金集中在遠期市場預
期殖利率仍有望下探、貨幣相對具備韌性的少數例外國家。
✓ 美元信用債落袋為安或轉向防禦，提防利差反彈風險：不建議再盲目追高新興市場美元信用債 ，因為OAS已收窄至歷史底部，

未來利差再收窄的空間極為有限 ，反而面臨金融環境一旦轉緊、利差大幅彈升的下行風險。建議投資人適度逢高獲利了結 ，
或將資金轉向存續期間較短的優質高信評債券，以降低殖利率波動與存續期間帶來的資本損失。

顧 • 不局限於美股，AI熱潮全面推高全球股市：今年以來，全球 AI 相關股票漲幅高達46.4%，優於非 AI 股票10.6%的表現。本
輪漲勢主要由半導體及硬體板塊的復甦所驅動，軟體與加速器相關企業的表現則相對平穩。值得注意的是，AI 股票的領漲效
應已不再侷限於美股，韓國與台灣市場亦成為驅動全球 AI 板塊成長的主要貢獻者。
• 全球AI股票的估值仍低於其歷史水平：目前AI股票的預期本益比約為21.5倍，仍低於過去五年平均的24.4倍，顯示市場對AI
成長前景的評價尚未完全恢復。若從相對估值角度觀察，AI股票相較非AI股票及整體市場的溢價亦明顯收斂。目前AI股票相
對非AI股票的估值溢價約35%，低於五年平均的61%；相較於全球基準MSCI世界指數，其估值溢價為18%，亦遠低於歷史

平均的41%。在AI獲利成長動能持續兌現的情況下，未來仍具備進一步估值修復與重評價的潛力。

顧 • AI股票驅動全球獲利成長：從成分股數量來看，AI相關公司僅占MSCI全球指數的3.9%，但在2026年，這些公司預計將貢獻

速（15%）的四倍。
• 雲服務巨頭主導資本支出，全球成長動能強勁：彭博預估，相關半導體企業的銷售成長率將由2025年的25%躍升至2026年
的63%，硬體企業則將由18%加速至37%。儘管半導體與硬體供應鏈展現強勁成長前景，雲服務巨頭仍是AI基礎設施建設背
後的核心出資方。分析顯示，雲服務巨頭的資本支出在2025年已突破4,000億美元，五年內成長超過兩倍，預計至2027年將
上看1兆美元，象徵雲端與AI正進入長期結構性成長階段，在基本面展望與資金面支撐下，全球AI供應鏈有望維持偏多格局。

• 短線漲多，股市波動度放大：美國6月初公布的非農就業數據意外強勁，而且隨後公布的消費者物價指數年增率升至3年來新
高，讓市場開始擔憂聯準會貨幣政策有可能會轉為升息，半導體類股出現獲利了結賣壓。儘管6月初費城半導體指數出現單
日大跌10%的恐慌拋售，但劇烈波動是基於過去幾週的融漲，中長期來看漲多修正有助股市多頭表現。
• 期中選舉年股市波動大，選後行情佳：美國期中選舉通常為金融市場帶來週期性規律，歷史數據顯示，期中選舉前約 6~12
個月，股市通常表現較疲弱，常出現10~20%的回檔，主要反應各種政策的不確定性，讓市場普遍呈現觀望立場。然而自
1950年以來，選後一年美股大多呈現正報酬，標普500指數平均報酬約為12%~15%，顯示市場並不那麼在意國會席次的結
果，而是在不確定性消除後，有助於判斷各產業的投資前景。

• 觀點：當前市場雖受利率、通膨與地緣政治影響而波動加劇，但整體仍具中長期成長動能。短期震盪屬正常修正，反而提供
布局機會，建議投資人把握回檔時點，分批配置具長期成長潛力的優質資產，以迎接下一波市場行情。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, May
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
15:25 June 09 2026 Share
Moderation in cost pressures sees business pessimism soften, while conditions held
steady. Confidence +10pts to -14 / Conditions unchanged at +3.
The May NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 May, capturing more of a reaction to
recent developments in the Middle East and the Government’s FY2027 Federal Budget. Business

confidence continued to retrace losses following March’s sharp decline, rising 10pts to -14, likely

### Page 2

supported by an easing in cost pressures in the month (discussed below). Despite the improvement,
confidence remains 18pts below its long-run average – a deeply pessimistic level underscoring the
ongoing impact of heightened uncertainty. Consumers continue to share this deep sense of pessimism,
with cost-of-living pressures a clear concern for many households.
It was encouraging to see a moderation in cost pressures in May. Most notably, purchase cost growth

declined by 1.8ppt – the sharpest monthly fall on record – to 2.6%qtr. However, this represents only a
partial reversal from the striking surge over the previous two months, with purchase cost growth still
sitting 1.4ppt above its long-run average. Labour cost growth also eased modestly, down 0.2ppt to
1.5%qtr, reversing last month’s increase. Consistent with the moderation in input costs, final product
price growth declined by 0.8ppt to 0.9%qtr – also the largest fall on record – but again, still 0.4ppt

above its long-run average. This is broadly consistent with an ongoing, albeit moderated, risk of cost
pass-through.
The headline business conditions index held steady at 3pts in May, following a 3pts decline in April.
Conditions remain 4pts below their long-run average and well below the peak of 11pts recorded in
October last year. On a three-month average basis, conditions continue to trend lower through 2026,
reinforcing the view that underlying momentum has been easing, as evinced in the latest National

Accounts.
Across the major sub-components, profitability moved into negative territory, declining 1pt to -1,

### Page 3

indicating that more firms are reporting a deterioration in profitability than an improvement. This leaves
the sub-index 6pts below its long-run average and suggests that the higher cost base continues to
squeeze margins. Trading conditions improved modestly, rising 1pt to 8pts, while the employment sub-
index held firm at 2pts (+1pts when rounding), supporting our view that some of the outsized weakness
in the April Labour Force data reflected abnormal seasonality.

Capacity utilisation declined notably to 81.9% in May, its lowest level in 12 months. While still 0.5ppt
above its long-run average, this outcome reflects a clear easing since the onset of the conflict in the
Middle East. By contrast, capital expenditure rebounded, rising 6pts to 10 and re-establishing a
position 4pts above its long-run average, consistent with the strength in capex intentions evident in the
March quarter capex survey.
Among other indicators, forward orders improved, rising 5pts to 0, in line with the modest pickup in

trading conditions. Inventories declined by 1pt to 3, likely reflecting weaker forward orders in the
previous month.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
June 8, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Midterms Q&A: The Market’s Next Big Hurdle: With less than six
months to go, the midterm elections are coming into sharper focus. No two election cycles
are created equal, but history may be a useful guide as the season unfolds. Historically,
increased volatility has been typical ahead of election season, but reacting to it has rarely

paid. While the S&P 500 often experienced modest gains and seasonally weak months
during midterm years, the real story emerges once political uncertainty fades into the
rearview. The months following an election regularly brought a pronounced rebound
regardless of the political party in power. For markets, profits matter more than politics.
For investors, the implication is straightforward—tune out the election, the noise, stay
focused on fundamentals, and maintain a long-term perspective regardless of how the

political winds shift.
Market View—Mega IPOs: Some Thoughts on Trading Dynamics, Liquidity and
Retail Access: A pipeline of three high-profile initial public offerings (IPO)—primarily
within the Artificial Intelligence (AI) and broader Technology and Telecommunications
sectors—is expected in mid-2026, likely attracting significant investor attention and
media focus. While the headline valuations are sizable, market structure and post-IPO
trading dynamics suggest the immediate impact on broader portfolios may be more

contained than anticipated. At the same time, alternative access routes such as Special
Purpose Vehicles (SPV) for qualified investors, private vehicles, and exchange-traded
funds (ETF) introduce additional structural, valuation, and regulatory considerations that
require careful assessment. Overall, our outlook remains cautiously constructive: The
market appears capable of absorbing the new issuance, but near-term performance
is likely to be influenced more by supply-demand dynamics and technical factors than

underlying fundamentals, with potential for increased volatility.
Thought of the Week— What Do Shifting Rate Expectations Mean for the U.S.
Debt Maturity Wall?: Since the start of the U.S./Iran conflict on February 28, the market
has gone from pricing in 2 to 3 interest rate cuts this year to seriously entertaining the
possibility of a rate hike. Where rates are headed matters not just for rate-sensitive areas
of the economy like housing and construction but also for the wall of U.S. government

debt set to mature. Indeed, owing to massive pandemic-era borrowing, $9 trillion
must be refinanced through year-end at higher rates. Meanwhile, the U.S. Treasury’s
preference for shorter-term issuance suggests refinancing needs may remain elevated.
Inflation will be key to watch this summer, though the good news is that long-run
inflation expectations are still well-anchored and demand for U.S. debt stands strong.
Though not our base case, an unexpected shift in Federal Reserve (Fed) policy would

pose risks not only to growth but also to America’s debt interest burden. We continue to
urge diversification in portfolios given shifting expectations.

2 of 8 June 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Midterms Q&A: The Market’s Next Big Hurdle
Emily Avioli, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Jordy Fuentes, Wealth Management Analyst
Amid this year’s relentless news cycle, investors could be forgiven for overlooking an
important event on the horizon: The midterm elections are fast approaching. With less
than six months to go, however, it may be time to bring them into sharper focus.

Volatility around midterm election years isn’t unusual, but this cycle may carry a heightened
degree of investor unease. U.S. voters are heading into elections contending with elevated
gas prices, inflation concerns, and a deepening affordability squeeze—all factors that
could have meaningful electoral consequences. While no two elections are created equal,
history may be a useful guide to help frame investor expectations as the season unfolds.
How much volatility should investors brace for? Midterm election years have

historically exhibited higher volatility than other years, with the divergence becoming more
pronounced over the summer and building into the final months before the election (Exhibit
1A). The Chicago Board Options Exchange Volatility Index confirms the pattern—volatility
has generally built in the months before elections and has notably declined once political
uncertainty is in the rearview.
1 While the data suggests that markets could get choppy as

we inch closer to election day, it’s worth noting that market corrections of 10% or more
have occurred in only 29% of midterm election years since 1928. Midterm election years
tend to be the weakest of any year out of the four-year presidential cycle, but, since 1928,
the S&P 500 Index has still seen an annual total return of 7.1%, on average.
How can midterms influence seasonality trends? Compared to nonelection years,
midterm years have been weaker in almost every month. September stands out as the

laggard—since 1928, the S&P 500 has averaged a 1% loss and was higher only 50%
of the time.
2 As political uncertainty begins to resolve, however, markets tended to
experience a pronounced Q4 rebound. The S&P 500 has typically found its footing in
October, advancing 67% of the time with an average total return of 2.5% (Exhibit 1B).
Overall, S&P 500 Q4 total returns have averaged 6.5%, compared to a muted 0.2% total
return during Q1, Q2, and Q3.
3

A similar seasonal dynamic has been observed in Small-caps, with the Russell 2000
demonstrating a late-year recovery: November has been the strongest month for Small-
caps in midterm years, delivering an average total return of 3.2% and positive outcomes
82% of the time.
4 Q4 performance for the Russell 2000 Index has historically averaged
6.5%, marking a sharp inflection from the -1.9% decline recorded on average over Q1,
Q2, and Q3. Ultimately, Small-caps have seen a slightly negative average annual total

return of -0.4% in midterm election years since 1982.
How does 2026 compare so far? Year-to-date (YTD) performance is already above
annual midterm year averages, with Small-caps up 14.8% and Large-caps up 8.4%.
5
While defensives have modestly outperformed cyclicals during past midterm election
years, 2026 has diverged from this pattern so far—the top-performing S&P 500 sectors
YTD are Energy and Information Technology, with respective total returns of 29.2%

and 17.1%. It could also be argued that some of the volatility typically observed in the
second half of midterm election years has been pulled forward this year, with the S&P
500 already experiencing a peak-to-trough drawdown of 9.1% in 2026. This reinforces
a key point: Every election cycle is unique. While history can provide helpful context, it is
not a reliable predictor of market outcomes.
1 Bloomberg. Refers to average VIX level around midterm elections from 1990 – 2022.

2 Bloomberg. Total returns referenced. Data as of June 1, 2026.
3 Bloomberg, average quarterly performance between Q1 and Q3 of midterm election years. Data as of May 29, 2026. Total
returns referenced.
4 Bloomberg. June 2, 2026. Includes all available data from 1982 – 2022.
5 Bloomberg. May 29, 2026. Total returns referenced.
Investment Implications
If history is a guide, there could be
more episodic volatility ahead as
midterm election season approaches. In

our view, any related market weakness
could represent a potential buying
opportunity for long-term investors.

### Page 3

3 of 8 June 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Does it really matter who wins? Midterm election outcomes can matter at the margin
for S&P 500 returns. Equities generally favor divided government, as sweep scenarios
elevate the risk of abrupt and disruptive policy shifts. Since 1928, the S&P 500 has
delivered an average annual total return of 11.4% under a unified Congress, versus
13.5% when Congress is divided. That said, a split Congress can come with its own

set of challenges. Legislative gridlock can complicate negotiations surrounding issues
like government funding and the debt ceiling, presenting another flavor of episodic
volatility and headline risk. No matter which party wins the election, avoid falling for
the misleading idea that the outcome will dramatically change the market’s direction—
remember, profits matter more than politics.
What happens in the year after the election? On this point, the data is clear: Post-

midterm election periods have typically been very constructive for Equities. In all available
data since 1928, the S&P 500 has risen 92% of the time in the 12 months following
the midterm election. Narrowing the data to just the post-war period, the S&P 500 has
delivered positive total returns 100% of the time over both 6- and 12-month horizons,
averaging 14.9% and 18.3%, respectively (Exhibit 1C). The year after the midterm has
also historically been the strongest in the four-year presidential cycle, with an average

annual total return of 17.6% since 1928 (Exhibit 1D). When it comes to midterm election
volatility, investors have historically reaped the benefits from staying the course.

Bottom Line: Volatility heading into midterm elections is typical, but reacting to it rarely

pays. The surrounding noise, whether from media or political rhetoric, often overstates
the stakes. History suggests that long-term equity returns are driven by earnings, not
election outcomes. For investors, the implication is straightforward—tune out the
election, the noise, stay focused on fundamentals, and maintain a long-term perspective
regardless of how the political winds shift.

### Page 4

4 of 8 June 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Mega IPOs: Some Thoughts on Trading Dynamics,
Liquidity and Retail Access
Chief Investment Office, Investment Manager Selection Team
A significant pipeline of three high-profile IPOs—particularly within the AI, Technology
and Telecommunication ecosystem—is expected to roll out in mid-2026, generating
much investor interest and plenty of media hype.
We believe there are several aspects of these IPOs to consider with respect to portfolio

positioning and market impact. While aggregate market values are substantial, the
structure of market access and the mechanics of post-IPO trading suggest that near-
term market impact may be more limited than headline figures imply.
At the same time, evolving access to potential oppportunities or pathways—special
purpose vehicles SPVs, private access vehicles, ETFs—can carry meaningful structural,
valuation, and regulatory risks that warrant careful diligence.

Our view is constructive but cautious: While the market can likely absorb new issuance,
short-term dynamics will likely be driven more by supply constraints and technical
factors than by fundamentals. Near-term market volatility could be one outcome
of this backdrop.
Index Inclusion Has The Potential To Create Sequential,
Nonfundamental Demand
More indexes have introduced "fast entry" mechanisms that allow sizeable IPOs
to be included earlier in order to better present their intended exposure. Eligibility

requirements and inclusion timelines vary by index methodology, with some indexes
adding IPOs as early as 5 days, 10 days, or 15 days after listing. This compares to the
typical 3 to 12 month seasoning period for IPOs historically.
As a result, the sizeable nature of these IPOs are set to experience greater index related
demand than IPOs historically. However, most core indexes are weighted by a security's
free float adjusted market capitalization, not by total market capitalization, meaning

these securities will receive more modest index weights, even if the IPO is eligible for
early inclusion.
While the market values for the upcoming large IPO companies are very high at around
$3.5 trillion in total, the shares available to purchase and the impact on the market are
not likely to be significant initially. This is due to the expected size of the initial float,
which is expected to be in the range of 3% to 10%, depending on the company. This

level is lower than typical modern technology IPOs at 10% to 25% but not unusual for
high-demand IPOs.
Goldman Sachs Portfolio Strategy Research
6 has estimated that a company with a
$1 trillion market capitalization but only 5% free float would carry an index weighting
of roughly 0.1% in the S&P 500, 0.2% in the Russell Growth Index and 1.4% in the
Nasdaq-100. The Nasdaq 100 Index is a notable exception, as it uses a modified
market capitalization. As such, its inclusion decisions may be particularly important to

monitor as constituents could be weighted higher than their free float adjusted market
capitalization weight.
6 Goldman Sachs Portfolio Strategy Research, U.S. Weekly Kick Start: “The equity market impact of proposed index
inclusion rule changes”. As of March 20, 2026.
Investment Implications
While we believe the upcoming major
IPOs could set significant milestones
for company market values and provide
public access to several innovation
leaders, the near-term impact on Equity

indexes and portfolios are likely to be
contained due to the limited availability
of shares and index inclusion guardrails.

### Page 5

5 of 8 June 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Expect front-loaded price strength and volatility, followed by normalization
as supply expands.
We believe there are several positives to be drawn from the small float, mostly tied
to volatility. First, the impact on stock(s) sold out of the index (to make room for
stocks added) will be manageable as portfolios are rebalanced. Two, the incremental
concentration to the AI/Technology theme will not be material in the near term. And

three, the market will be able to absorb the new shares in a fluid manner.
As with most IPOs, these stocks are likely to be highly sensitive to trading sentiments
in the near term or until supply expands. There will likely be upward pricing pressure on
the stocks when added to various indices. And high valuations for certain IPOs could also
exacerbate price volatility. Volatility should subside following lock-up periods, and insider
selling and secondary offerings increase share supply.

There are multiple strategies to access IPOs.
Along with the pipeline of high-profile IPOs, it is important to consider how to access
these companies. Given the ability to invest earlier in a company’s lifecycle at lower
valuations and with attractive deal structures, CIO would advocate qualified investors
getting IPO exposure through investing in Long-Only Growth Equity Funds or for
qualified investors via Venture Capital and Private Growth Equity Funds.

Retail access is now available in many structures including listed closed end funds
and for qualified investors, traditional Private Equity funds (which are potentially more
attractive because they are diversified but tend to have the highest allocation % to
these deals), evergreen private markets vehicles as well as interval funds and more
recently, SPVs.
However, strategies that require periodic liquidity are subject to premium/discount
volatility; in addition, highly concentrated, publicly traded strategies contain multiple

investment risks beyond the fundamental performance of the IPOs. And recently,
regulatory scrutiny has increased around “access vehicles” that seemingly offer exposure
to one or a basket of anticipated IPOs, but, in some cases, the offering entity has not
had a legal right to those shares.
Conclusion
We believe the upcoming major IPOs could set significant milestones for company
market values and provide public access to several innovation leaders, the near-term

impact on Equity Indexes and portfolios are likely to be contained due to the limited
availability of shares and index inclusion guardrails.
We continue to believe that some of the best ways to access the pre-IPO market is
through Private Equity and Venture Capital funds for qualified investors, including some
of the new evergreen/interval structures that carry the benefit of early entry valuation
and significantly less volatility. When considering an allocation to these IPOs, it is

important to note that many clients have significant exposure to the Technology sector
in both public and private solutions given recent markets, so any allocation to individual
companies should be sized appropriately.

### Page 6

6 of 8 June 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
What Do Shifting Rate Expectations Mean for the U.S. Debt
Maturity Wall?
Ariana Chiu. Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the start of the U.S./Iran conflict on February 28, the market has gone from pricing
in 2 to 3 rate cuts this year to seriously entertaining the possibility of a rate hike. Where
rates are headed matters not only for rate-sensitive areas of the economy like housing

and construction but also for the wall of U.S. government debt set to mature this year
and beyond. Owing to massive pandemic-era borrowing at near-zero interest rates, $9
trillion in government debt must be refinanced by year-end, now at rates closer to 3.5%
to 4% (Exhibit 2A and B).

Exhibit 2A) *Refers to June through end of year. 2056+ indicates 2056 or later. Source: Bloomberg. Data as of June 2, 2026. Exhibit 2B) Source: Bloomberg. Data as of April 30, 2026.

Refinancing ample outstanding debt at higher rates means higher interest costs, hardly
a source of comfort when Uncle Sam already spends more on interest payments than
national defense. Meanwhile, the U.S. Treasury’s preference for shorter-term issuance
suggests refinancing needs are likely biased higher in years to come; as it stands, 22%
of outstanding debt is in Treasury bills (maturity of one year or less).
The path of inflation remains key to watch as we enter the summer months. The good

news is that long-run inflation expectations are still well anchored, moderating in recent
weeks to pre-war levels. In the meantime, the bond market may have already done
some of the heavy lifting for the Fed; Bloomberg Economics estimated at the end of
May that the roughly 50 basis-point (bps) move in the 10-year Treasury yield since the
start of the war had resulted in tighter financial conditions equivalent to roughly 75 bps
of rate hikes.
7
The most important question is whether investors will continue to demand U.S. debt. The

answer is still a resounding yes, in our view. Indeed, for all the geopolitical volatility and
uncertainty of the last year, demand for U.S. assets has far from waned thanks to the
depth of its financial markets, the dollar’s reserve currency status, and strong economic
fundamentals. Foreign holdings of U.S. Treasurys, just one of many drivers of demand,
reached a record $9.5 trillion during Q1 according to the U.S. Treasury.
Looking ahead, a major change in Fed policy (e.g., unanticipated swift rate hikes) would

be cause for concern, not only for growth but also for the effective interest burden on
outstanding debt which has remained relatively flat thanks to older, lower-rate debt.
That’s not our base case, and we remain watchful of inflation and labor inputs in the
months to come. Potentially shifting rate expectations only further reinforces the
importance of diversification across and within asset classes in portfolios.
7 “Bond Market Hanads Warsh 75-bps Fed Rate Hike Already,” Bloomberg, May 28, 2026.

Investment Implications
The path of inflation and interest rates
warrants close monitoring this summer
given the Iran conflict, AI supply chain
strains, and resilient economic growth.
Our base case continues to be that the
Fed remains on hold this year.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Total Return is a performance benchmark that measures the index's price changes and assumes all cash dividends are reinvested.
Russell 2000 Index tracks the performance of the 2000 largest U.S. companies, serving as a key benchmark for mega-cap and ultra-large-cap equities.
Chicago Board Options Exchange Volatility Index is a real-time market index that measures the expected 30-day forward-looking volatility of the U.S. stock market.

Russell Growth Index measures the performance of US large cap growth stocks.
Nasdaq 100 Index includes 100 of the largest domestic and international non-financial companies listed on The Nasdaq Stock Market based on market capitalization.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 208: Consumer Checkpoint_ Sunny days Consumers remain resilient, as lower- and middle-income households’ spending and wage growth accelerates. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Sunny days Consumers remain resilient, as lower- and middle-income households’ spending and wage growth accelerates. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-06-01
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

12983023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Sunny days 
11 June 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending momentum is remarkably robust, with total card spending rising 5.1% year-over-year (YoY) in May - the 
strongest growth in nearly four years, according to Bank of America internal card data. Moreover, growth isn't just gasoline-
driven - underlying spending remains firm across both goods and services. 
• Notably, the gap across income cohorts has also narrowed significantly in both spending and wage growth, but some of this 
could reflect the impact of FIFA World Cup 2026™, so we remain cautious about whether this convergence will persist into the 
latter part of 2026. Additionally, while we see little sign of shifts in consumer behavior that would suggest weakness, some 
consumers are making more trips to the store, perhaps on the hunt for bargains. 
• Consumer financial health remains strong, with no clear signs of households resorting to borrowing to support spending. While 
the savings rate has fallen, households' level of savings is relatively elevated. And the seasonal upswing in deposit balances from 
tax refunds has been larger in 2026 than it was in 2025.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
Making hay in May 
Consumer spending remains resilient, with little sign that higher gas prices have dented momentum yet. In May, Bank of America 
total aggregated credit and debit card spending per household increased 5.1% year-over-year (YoY) – the largest gain in nearly 
four years – following the 4.8% YoY rise in April (Exhibit 1). While higher YoY gasoline spending is contributing to this strength, 
total spending excluding gas still rose 3.9% YoY in May, only a small easing from 4.0% YoY in April. 
Seasonally-adjusted (SA) spending per household rose 0.1% month-over-month (MoM) in May, following a 0.5% increase in April. 
This follows gains of 0.6% and 0.9% MoM in the prior two months.  
Exhibit 1: Both YoY spending growth excluding gas and MoM spending eased in May

right-hand side (rhs))

One emerging trend: retail (excluding gasoline and restaurants) is contributing almost the same amount toward overall spending 
growth as services including restaurants (Exhibit 2). Notably, this is first time this has happened since February 2021, when retail 
spending outpaced services spending as consumers remained socially distanced during the pandemic. Retail categories showing 
growth in the last month include electronics, furniture and clothing.  
Exhibit 2: Retail and gasoline are contributing a significant amount towards overall spending growth 
Contribution to YoY total credit and debit card spending growth by category, based on Bank of America card data (monthly, SA, percentage points contribution)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Within services, overall spending is being driven more by discretionary than by necessity spending. Travel and tourism and 
restaurant spending have made particularly strong contributions to discretionary services, while leisure has contributed less 
(Exhibit 3). In our view, FIFA World Cup 2026™ might help support further growth in discretionary services over the summer 
months. Meanwhile, necessity services spending (e.g., rent and utilities) has eased somewhat relative to 2025, partly reflecting a 
declining contribution from TV and communications. It is possible that some of the necessity spending shifts may reflect 
migration to and from card spending to other payment channels particularly in rent and communication spending (Exhibit 4).

Any signs of a pullback?  
Some consumers may be hunting for bargains   
Beneath the remarkably robust overall spending picture, are there any signs of weakness in consumer behavior? Largely, the 
answer appears to be “no.”  
However, when looking at how frequently consumers shop each month, we note a pickup in the number of transactions made at 
general merchandise stores. At the same time, spending has not increased proportionally, which may suggest consumers are 
being more selective and making more frequent trips in search of bargains.  
There are also some signs in clothing and food services: around 1% of households may have stopped transacting altogether. This 
would be consistent with a small subset of consumers facing greater pressure and forgoing these discretionary items. However, 
the share of consumers doing so appears, in our view, to be very small (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: April showers). 
Exhibit 5: Consumers may be hunting for bargains at general merchandise stores 
Spend per card transaction, card transaction per household per month (three-month moving average, % YoY) and households transacting as a proportion of card 
total sample (three-month moving average percentage point change YoY)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Another place to look for behavioral change is around “ticket size,” i.e. are consumers deliberately spending less on more 
expensive items? When we look across categories, we see little sign of this sort of behavior. Indeed, in airline spending the 
opposite is true: higher airfares mean consumers are having to transact more at higher price points (Exhibit 6 and Exhibit 7). 
Only in electronics is there evidence of consumers gravitating to smaller ticket sizes in the past year.

One very large ticket item is a car. Gasoline prices directly impact the running costs of internal combustion engine vehicles, so 
it’s possible consumers are pulling back in response to higher fuel costs. However, when we look at larger auto-related consumer 
payments as a proxy for down payments on new vehicles, we see no clear signs of retrenchment (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 8: Auto sales remain robust across income cohorts

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
And lower- and middle-income households are weaker  
While we see few signs of behavioral change in response to the gas price shock, we do continue to observe a divergence in 
spending growth across the household income distribution. But encouragingly, the gap between spending and wage growth 
across higher- and lower-income groups has narrowed.  
In May, card spending rose 4.1% YoY for lower-income households and 4.3% YoY for middle-income households, while higher-
income households saw stronger growth of 5.4% YoY (Exhibit 9). As a result, the gap between higher-income and other 
households narrowed to its lowest level since June 2025.  
At the same time, we have seen a similar narrowing in the after-tax wage gap, according to Bank of America customer deposit 
data (Exhibit 10). In May, after-tax wage growth for higher-income households slowed to 5.6% YoY from 5.9% YoY in April. 
Meanwhile, lower-income households’ after-tax wage growth rose to 3.1% – the highest rate since January 2025 – while middle-
income households’ wage growth rose to 3.5% YoY.  
Exhibit 9: Higher-income households’ spending rose to 5.4% YoY in

In our view, one potential driver of the improvement in lower-income households’ spending and wage growth may be the labor

by 172K in May. 
One note of caution: some of this job growth could reflect hiring in service industries ahead of FIFA World Cup 2026™. When we 
look at our estimate of payroll growth split across household income cohorts, it appears the most significant uptick has come 
from lower-income households which could indicate higher-touch service sector job growth. If this is the case, while labor 
momentum could persist for several more months, it may prove slower after the summer (Exhibit 12),

It could be that some consumers are feeling less inclined to save partly because of significant wealth gains from rises in 
equities. But it’s also worth noting that, in Bank of America deposit and card data, households’ wages were growing faster than 
their card spending across income cohorts in May of last year, whereas in May 2026 only higher-income households were in this 
position. Lower- and middle-income households, on the other hand, are now seeing their spending growth outstrip their wage 
gains (Exhibit 20). This could, in our view, be somewhat pressuring these cohorts to pull back on saving.

relative to 2025, the rise in deposit balances has been greater this year between January and May across income cohorts. In our 
view, this should offer continued support to household spending over the coming months.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be 
considered in the context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may 
provide directional and/or predictive value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized 
selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with 
a deposit account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small 
Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. 
Bank of America per Small Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit 
account or a Small Business credit card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include 
business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to 
periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following 
reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This 
could be due to an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America 
cards in the month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to 
underlying economic momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during 
COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data 
from Bank of America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever 
median household savings and checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a 
consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month 
of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only 
consumer card holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate 
cards are excluded. Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization 
Code (MCC) defined by financial services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the 
North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending 
data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to childcare, rent, insurance, insurance, 
public transportation, and tax payments. Discretionary services includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, 
entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail includes but is not limited to general merchandise, 
miscellaneous, clothing, electronics, furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as 
items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual spending on the category.  
Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
For analysis looking at higher value transactions (including durables), we consider a value per transaction threshold estimated 
with reference to the top 30% of transactions by value in 2024. The share of higher value transactions is then the number of 
transactions above this threshold as a percentage of total transactions over time.

### Page 9

11 June 2026    9

I NSTI TUTE

Participant Pulse monitors plan participants’ behavior in Bank of America clients’ employee benefits programs, which comprise 
more than 4 million total participants with positive account balances as of March 31, 2026. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer 
deposit account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are 
bucketed according to terciles, with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower 
income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between 
these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, 
changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The income and tercile in which a household is 
categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Major grocery categories include sugar and sweets, juices and other non-alcoholic beverages, bakery products, processed fruits 
and vegetables, fresh fruit and vegetables, coffee and tea, fats and oils, milk, cereal and cereal products, other, cheese, and 
meats, poultry and fish, Other includes soups, snacks, frozen and freeze-dried prepared foods, and spices, seasonings, and 
condiments.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows:  
1. Gen Z, born after 1995 
2. Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995  
3. Older Millennials: born between 1978-1988 
4. Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977 
5. Baby Boomer: 1946-1964 
6. Traditionalists: pre-1946 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include 
purchases of non-gas items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 242: Germany’s reform train is picking up steam

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany’s reform train is picking up steam.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 22 June 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  22 June 2026   GERMANY
Germany’s reform train is picking up
steam
Proposals to reform the German pension system are clearly a good step
in the right direction. It s now up to the government to show its own
reform elan and implement the plans as quickly as possible
Fans flood into Fröttmaning for a World Cup match - a surge not unlike the demographic wave facing
Germany’s pension system

Germany can still win its opening World Cup matches and, it seems, still deliver reform. That's
the main takeaway from the weekend. For the first time since 2006, the national team started
the tournament with two wins, keeping faint hopes of a title and a broader turnaround alive.
Now, economic reforms may be following suit.
Officially, the so‑called pension commission, a 13‑member task force appointed by the
governing coalition, will present its proposals on Tuesday. News reports on Saturday detailed

33 proposals to reform Germany’s pension system. The government now has to adopt and
implement these proposals. Parliament will also have a say.
Earlier, some government members suggested that a full reform package, including healthcare
and tax reforms, would be presented before the parliamentary summer break next Tuesday.
We remain sceptical that the government can deliver such broad measures in that timeframe.
The proposals unveiled so far risk being too little, and potentially too late. Still, there are clear

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 22 June 2026 2
signs that reform is gaining momentum.
Last year's reform was a patch, not a fix
To understand the scale of today’s pension‑reform push, you have to rewind to December –
when the governing coalition nearly came apart over what some members saw as the first,
tentative push for change. Others saw it as little more than a costly extension of the status
quo. Ultimately, the government agreed to keep the statutory pension level locked at 48% of

average wages through 2031, rather than allowing it to drift down toward 45% under the
previous formula. This means workers who contributed to the statutory system for 45 years
and retire by 2031 will receive 48% of the average German wage as a state pension. Without
this decision, the level could have fallen to 44.9% by 2040.
That guarantee, however, does not come for free. Nothing involving 21 million pensioners and
a shrinking workforce ever does. The contribution rate is set to rise from 18.6% to 18.8% from

2027. The package also introduced a few quieter measures on 1 January: an expanded
"mother's pension" crediting childcare years more generously; a relaxation of the rules
preventing pensioners from returning to their old employer; and the so-called Aktivrente, a tax
break for people who work past the statutory retirement age of 67. Last year’s reform
amounted to little more than a pretence that the status quo could be extended forever.
Why a fix is needed

Meanwhile, pressure on the pension system continues to build like an avalanche. Germany's
old-age dependency ratio – people 65 and over relative to the working-age population – is set
to rise from 34% in 2024 to 51% by 2050. Put differently, in 1992, there were 2.7 working-age
people funding the pension of one retiree. In 2022, that fell to 2.0. By 2030, it will be just 1.5.
That's not a gentle slope. It's a step change, arriving now because the baby boomers are

retiring en masse this decade. The Federal Statistical Office's own December 2025 projections
put a fine point on it: one in four Germans will be 67 or older by 2035, up from one in five in
2024. And the number of people of pensionable age will keep climbing every year through
2038, adding 3.8 to 4.5 million to the system.
Without any changes, the pressure on public finances will become almost unbearable. As a
young civil servant, I helped to put the sustainability of public finances in ageing societies on

the agenda of European Council meetings. This was some 25 years ago. What sounded like
fiction to some back then has become a hard reality. Already, the German government is
spending more than two-thirds of its annual budget on health care and the pension system.
Last year’s decision was a patch. The system is in urgent need of a fix.
The proposals
This is why the government set up an independent task force with a mandate to analyse the

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 22 June 2026 3
system from the ground up. It will deliver recommendations covering not just the statutory
pillar but also occupational and private pensions. As mentioned, the commission put forward
33 recommendations, supported by a broad, though not unanimous, consensus.
The headline change: the retirement age will be linked to life expectancy from 2032 onwards.
The idea is a "two-to-one" rule, meaning that for every additional year of life expectancy,

people work eight months longer and draw a pension for four more months. Run that through
current demographic projections, and it results in a retirement age of 67.5 by 2041 and 68 by
2051. It means roughly half a year added per decade. It will be reviewed periodically rather
than locked in.
Early retirement without deductions after 45 years of contributions would be abolished under
the proposal. That benefit has been politically untouchable for the SPD in the past. It remains

to be seen how this proposal will be received by the broader SPD, beyond the government
members.
These initial proposals are clearly aimed at extending the working life, while other measures
seek to broaden the contributor base. Members of parliament and freelancers would be
integrated into the statutory state pension system. The commission didn’t (dare to) tackle the
question of how to deal with civil servants, beyond suggesting a reduction in their numbers.

It also proposes limiting the so-called €600 jobs – currently exempt from social contributions –
to students. These are important sources of employment in the retail sector. To be clear, these
jobs won’t disappear overnight, but they will become more expensive for employers.
One genuinely new idea is a mandatory, funded capital pillar explicitly modelled on Sweden's
premium pension. A slice of contributions, phased in from 0.5% of gross wages to 2% and split

evenly between employer and employee, would be invested in capital markets through a
state-run fund and paid out individually. The aim is to use market returns to modestly raise
the pension level above where the current pay-as-you-go formula alone would take it.
It's a first fix, but regaining competitiveness needs more
All in all, the reform proposals include important elements. Case in point: linking the retirement
age to life expectancy and establishing a new market-based pillar, even if the commission

could’ve been bolder and more ambitious. The positive take is that the reform train is picking
up speed. Still, this is no high‑speed train, but rather a fragile old model.
Amid all the enthusiasm, let's not forget that these changes are a bare necessity to contain
pressure on public finances. They will not, by themselves, make the German economy more
competitive. For that, the government will have to do more.
To return to the World Cup analogy: winning two matches and reaching the next round should

be the bare minimum for Germany’s national team. Bringing trophies home will require more

### Page 4

## Document 267: May CPI – underlying pressures firm (2026-06-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/May CPI – underlying pressures firm (2026-06-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-06-25
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia#inflation
May CPI – underlying pressures firm
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
18:00 June 24 2026 Share
Monthly May CPI –0.7%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.4%mth/3.6%yr.
May CPI fell –0.7%mth/4.0%yr, below both our forecast and market expectations, with the downside
surprise concentrated in holiday travel and clothing & footwear.
Underlying inflation firmed, with trimmed mean inflation in line with our expectations at

0.4%mth/3.6%yr. The three-month annualised pace lifted to 3.8%.

### Page 2

The May CPI fell –0.7%mth to be up 4.0%yr. This was below our estimate of –0.3%mth/4.4%yr and
market expectations of –0.4%mth/4.3%yr. May is typically a seasonally softer month for the CPI. In
seasonally adjusted terms, the CPI dipped –0.1%mth, compared with our estimate of +0.2%mth.

The key drags on headline inflation were transport (–0.4ppts), with fuel prices falling 11.9% in the
month, clothing & footwear (–0.1ppt) and recreation & culture (–0.4ppts). The latter two accounted for
nearly all of the miss in our monthly result, with domestic travel also softer than anticipated. Balancing
this was strong outcome in the housing component, which added+0.1ppt, with a broad-based pick-up
across electricity, rents and new dwelling costs.

While headline inflation was softer than projected, trimmed mean inflation came in-line with our
forecast, rising 0.4%mth/3.6%yr (slightly softer at the second decimal place). The three-month
annualised rate has picked up to 3.8% from 3.1% in February, before the breakout of the Middle East
conflict.

Housing remains the key inflation pressure point, with new dwelling costs posting their strongest
monthly increase since late-2022 and rents also surprising on the upside. There is also stronger
evidence that pressures are broadening particularly within services.
While oil and commodity prices have eased from recent highs, we expect further pass-through into
consumer prices over coming months as earlier cost increases continue to filter through the
economy. This is likely to keep inflation uncomfortably high for the RBA who will be concerned about
high inflation becoming embedded in domestic wage and price setting behaviour.

### Page 3

New dwelling costs rose by a stronger than expected0.9%mth/5.6%yr, the strongest monthly increase
since December 2022. The ABS again pointed to project home builders raising base prices to pass
through fuel surcharges and higher material costs.

We had flagged upside risks to this. Builder material price notifications data, showed a 17% in average
prices in May in seasonally adjusted terms, with data pointing to an easing to 11% in June and 9% in
August, but still well above average levels.  That points to ongoing pipeline pressure on build costs
ahead.

Rents were also firmer than we had expected at 0.4%mth. We also think there is further upside here
given trends in advertised rents which take some time to flow through to CPI rents.

At this stage, housing inflation remains the key pressure point. It accounts for around 20% of the CPI
basket and is typically a source of persistence for trimmed mean inflation, this keeps upside risk in
focus and is likely to keep the RBA uneasy.

### Page 4

Overall, food inflation came in largely as expected (0.6%mth vs 0.7%mth expected), however there
were some swings at the expenditure class level. Dairy products were a key upside surprise rising
2.3%mth. This likely reflected some temporary fuel surcharges announced by grocers across dairy
products. At the other end fruit & vegetables rose by less than we expected (1.1%mth vs 2.9%mth
expected). Food prices is an area we’ll be paying close attention to in H2. We are yet to see the impact
of higher fertiliser costs, which is having an impact on farmers planting decisions, and when combined
with an El Nino event could see larger food prices increases in late-Q3 onwards.

Market services excluding volatiles fell –0.3%mth, with the year-ended rate lifting to 3.2%yr. Excluding
volatiles and holiday travel, prices rose 0.2%mth/4.2%yr, in-line with expectations. Areas that showed
more pronounced increases include: audio, visual and computing & media services
(1.2%mth/10.5%yr); restaurant meals (0.4%mth/3.9%yr, takeaway foods was also strong
0.5%mth/4.1%yr); dwelling and motor vehicle repair costs (0.7%mth/3.7%yr and 0.9%mth/6.3%yr
respectively). All of these are running at their highest annual pace in the full monthly series and there is
risk of further upside given our expectations for stronger wage outcomes in H2 2026.

### Page 5

Overall, underlying pressures are broadening after showing more contained signs in March and April.
The share of the CPI basket that is running above 3% at a three-month annualised pace is just under
50%, after recording low-40s reads the past two months. This is also the highest share since around
October 2025.

The May data provide a stronger signal that second-order effects from the Middle East supply shock
are becoming more visible across consumer prices. Oil prices are off their highs and policy is still
dampening parts of fuel inflation, for now, but higher fuel, transport and commodity costs are continuing
to feed through to a broader range of categories. This is consistent with firms passing on at least part of
the increase in input costs, including energy, freight, plastics and chemicals.

The key question is whether these price increases reverse as cost pressures ease, or whether they
persist. Postal costs fell in May, as the fuel surcharge was reduced, showing some pass-through can
unwind. But the risk is that pass-through in other areas is stickier, particularly across services. The
increase in hairdressing prices, for example, could reflect firms adjusting prices not just for higher fuel
and transport costs, but for a broader expectation of higher operating costs. That makes the May
outcome less about whether pass-through is occurring, and more about how broad and persistent it
becomes.

The May data reinforce the RBA’s concern that inflation remains too high and that a period of slower
growth will be needed to return inflation to target. The post-meeting statement had already given a
stronger steer than recent communication, with the Board adding that it was prepared to increase the
cash rate further “if required”. Governor Bullock repeated this point in the media conference. That
looked deliberate, and suggests the Board wanted to push back against speculation that it is done
hiking.

Arena 将独⽴于 Facebook 和 Instagram 运营，涵盖政治、体育、娱乐等预测领域，初期采
⽤积分⽽⾮真实货币以规避监管⻛险，但不排除未来引⼊真实货币。此举折射出⾏业集体
转向预测市场的趋势，但 CFTC 对相关合约的法律定性仍悬⽽未决。
摘要：
Meta 正在押注预测市场这⼀新兴赛道。
6 ⽉ 23 ⽇，据《纽约时报》报道，这家社交媒体巨头正在秘密开发⼀款名为 "Arena" 的预测市场
应⽤，直接对标 Polymarket 和 Kalshi ，试图在这⼀因 2024 年美国⼤选⽽爆发式增⻓的领域分⼀
杯羹。
报道称，知情⼈⼠透露，该产品⽬前被公司内部定性为 " 实验性项⽬ " ，但同时也是 " 最⾼优先
级 " 任务。Arena 将独⽴于 Facebook 和 Instagram 运营，允许⽤⼾对政治、体育、娱乐及国际事
务等未来事件作出预测。与 Polymarket 等平台不同， Arena 初期可能采⽤类似电⼦游戏的积分体
系⽽⾮真实货币，但 Meta 尚未排除未来引⼊真实货币押注的可能性。

分析指出，Arena 的⼊局，折射出整个⾏业向预测市场集体转向的⼤趋势。 Coinbase 、
Kraken 、 Robinhood 等主流交易平台均已相继布局事件合约产品，Meta 此举意在抢占这⼀快速
扩张的市场。然⽽，预测市场在监管层⾯⾯临的不确定性依然突出，美国商品期货交易委员会
（ CFTC ）对相关合约的法律定性⾄今悬⽽未决。
Polymarket 效应：预测市场⾛⼊主流视野
Arena 的开发背景，与预测市场近年来的爆发式增⻓密不可分。
2024 年美国总统⼤选期间，基于加密货币的预测平台 Polymarket 迎来历史性突破 —— ⼤量交易
者涌⼊该平台对选举结果下注，推动交易量达到数⼗亿美元规模，预测市场由此从⼩众⼯具跃
升为主流政治讨论的重要参照。这⼀现象引发了整个科技和⾦融⾏业的⾼度关注。
⼏乎与此同时， Coinbase 、 Kraken 等加密原⽣公司纷纷探索预测市场机会，零售券商
Robinhood 也推出了与政治和经济结果挂钩的事件合约产品。预测市场正在从边缘⾛向中⼼。

Arena 产品设计：积分先⾏，真实货币留有余地
据报道， Arena 在产品形态上有意与现有竞争对⼿形成差异化。
该应⽤将独⽴于 Meta 旗下的 Facebook 和 Instagram 运营，覆盖政治、体育、娱乐和国际事务等
多个预测领域。
在货币化机制上， Arena 初期预计采⽤类似电⼦游戏的积分体系，⽽⾮直接的现⾦押注 —— 这⼀
设计或有助于规避监管层⾯的直接冲突。不过， Meta 并未明确排除未来引⼊真实货币的可能
性，为产品的商业化路径保留了空间。
值得注意的是，这并⾮ Meta ⾸次涉⾜预测领域。 2020 年， Meta 曾推出名为 "Forecast" 的类似产
品，⿎励⽤⼾在新冠疫情初期对时事和新兴趋势作出预测，但该产品最终于 2022 年关闭。

SpaceX 债市⾸秀！获得近 900 亿美元，约 3.6 倍认购订单，低于今年以来投资级债券平均 4
倍的认购规模。同时本次发债资⾦扎堆涌向短期品种， 10 年期债券利差仍⽐英特尔⾼出 50

摘要：
SpaceX ⾸次进军债市吸引了近 900 亿美元的认购订单，但债券投资者对短期限品种的集中追捧
和⾼于同类企业的信⽤利差，折射出市场对公司⻓期现⾦流可持续性的深层疑虑。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露， SpaceX 周⼆以五档期限结构发⾏ 250 亿美元债券，为该公司⾸次公
开发债，共吸引近 900 亿美元认购订单。今年以来⾼评级债券交易的平均认购倍数约为发⾏规模
的四倍，SpaceX 的认购倍数约为 3.6 倍低于此。
然⽽，需求的结构颇值关注。另有知情⼈⼠表⽰，认购最为踊跃的是期限最短的⼀档，即⻛险
相对最低的品种。与此同时， SpaceX 为 10 年期债券⽀付的信⽤利差，在经过压缩后仍⽐评级相

近的英特尔同期限债券⾼出约 50 个基点。
这⼀市场格局揭⽰了股债投资者之间的根本分歧：股票投资者愿意为⻢斯克的宏⼤愿景⽀付溢
价，债券投资者则更关注偿债能⼒与现⾦流的安全边际。标普全球评级预测， SpaceX 将持续消
耗现⾦⾄ 2030 年，且明年烧钱速度将⼤幅提速。
周⼆ SpaceX 股价盘中⼀度上涨 5.6% ，最终收涨 0.98% ，终结此前连续三个交易⽇下跌的趋势。
此前三个交易⽇的连续下跌已令公司市值蒸发逾 6000 亿美元。
⾼溢价与短端偏好，信⽤市场态度审慎
SpaceX 此次 10 年期债券的价格指引较美国国债利差约在 25 ⾄ 140 个基点之间；即便经过收窄，
这⼀利差仍⽐评级相近的英特尔 10 年期债券⾼出约 50 个基点，反映出信⽤市场对这家公司的差
异化定价。
认购向短期限债券集中倾斜，在业内是⼀个有说明意义的信号。期限越短，投资者对远期偿债
能⼒的依赖程度越低。这⼀偏好结构，直接指向市场对 SpaceX 未来现⾦流⾛势的保留态度。

## Document 337: US Daily_ June FOMC Recap_ Hikes Look More Likely but Are Still Not Our Base Case

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Daily_ June FOMC Recap_ Hikes Look More Likely but Are Still Not Our Base Case.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-06-17
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

David Mericle 
+1(212)357-2619 | 
david.mericle@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
US Daily: June FOMC Recap: Hikes Look More Likely but Are Still Not Our 
Base Case
17 June 2026 | 7:58PM EDT
The June FOMC meeting delivered a hawkish surprise, with nine participants n
projecting a hike in 2026 (vs. our expectation of three) and the median 
projection for 2027 Q4/Q4 core PCE inflation rising to 2.5% (vs. our expectation 
of 2.3%).  Market pricing for the fed funds rate at the end of 2026 rose by 20bp 
in response to the projections and Chairman Kevin Warsh’s press conference. 
Today’s meeting raises the risk of interest rate hikes later this year, but our base n
case is still that the FOMC will leave the policy rate unchanged this year.  A 
majority could support a hike, perhaps for a range of different reasons, if 
upcoming inflation prints are uncomfortable and job growth remains solid.  But 
we suspect that most of the voters still lean toward an unchanged policy rate and 
that participants treated the news about a deal with Iran and the reopening of 
the Strait of Hormuz cautiously for now.  If so, today’s projections could prove 
stale if trade resumes, which should resolve the most serious source of upside 
risk to inflation fairly quickly. 
Warsh also announced that he is creating five task forces to reconsider Fed n
communications, the Fed’s balance sheet, the Fed’s use of and reliance on 
existing data sources, productivity and jobs in “an era of transformation,” and 
the Fed’s inflation frameworks.  He appeared particularly focused on rethinking 
Fed communications.  Fed officials have already discussed some of these topics 
recently, and it is hard to know if the task forces will reach different conclusions.

### Page 2

June FOMC Recap: Hikes Look More Likely but Are Still Not Our Base Case

As widely expected, the FOMC left the funds rate unchanged at 3.5-3.75% today and 
removed the previous forward guidance suggesting cuts from its statement at the June 
FOMC meeting today.  The FOMC also substantially shortened and simplified its 
post-meeting statement. 
But the meeting also delivered a significant hawkish surprise, with nine participants 
projecting a rate hike in 2026, against our expectation that just three would.  Because 
the new Chairman Kevin Warsh chose not to submit projections, this resulted in a nine to 
nine tie in the dot plot between those projecting at least one hike and those projecting 
an unchanged policy rate or, in one case, a rate cut. 
Consistent with the hawkish message from the dots, the key surprise in the economic 
projections was that the median forecast for 2027 Q4/Q4 core PCE inflation rose 0.3pp 
to 2.5%, above our expectation that it would rise more modestly to 2.3%.  This suggests 
that participants were more concerned that the recent firmer inflation news, caused in 
part by the energy cost shock from the war with Iran, has implications for inflation 
further down the road. 
It was not clear where Warsh stood on the likelihood of a hike being appropriate from 
the press conference.  He took a balanced view on whether the current stance of 
monetary policy is restrictive, noting that it is likely restraining housing activity but it is 
hard to say the same of the impact on financial markets.  But he repeatedly emphasized 
the Fed’s commitment to price stability, which might make him sympathetic to a hike if 
upcoming inflation reports are discouraging.  And he said that “financial market prices 
are probably the most important source of information to guide central bankers,” which 
might imply that he sees market pricing of a higher funds rate later this year as 
supporting the case for hiking. 
In response to both the projections and Warsh’s press conference, market pricing for the 
fed funds rate at the end of 2026 rose by 20bp and pricing for the end of 2027 rose by 
11bp (Exhibit 1). 
17 June 2026   2
Goldman Sachs US Daily

### Page 3

Today’s meeting raises the risk of interest rate hikes later this year, but our base case is 
still that the FOMC will leave the policy rate unchanged this year.  A majority could 
support a hike, perhaps for a range of different reasons, if upcoming inflation prints are 
uncomfortable and job growth remains solid.  Some might be more concerned about 
recent inflation trends, while others might be less convinced that a hike is necessary but 
see little harm from a starting point where job growth has been running strong.  A great 
deal will depend on upcoming inflation reports, and we do expect that May core PCE 
inflation will be firm at 31bp, though 12bp of that would come from financial services. 
But we suspect that a majority of the twelve voters, as opposed to the nineteen 
participants, still lean toward an unchanged policy rate.  We also suspect that 
participants treated the news about a deal with Iran and the reopening of the Strait of 
Hormuz cautiously for now.  If so, today’s projections could prove stale if trade resumes, 
which should resolve the most serious source of upside risk to inflation fairly quickly.  
Warsh noted that “my colleagues around the table, when they submitted their dots, 
understand the world is changing quite quickly” and will not feel bound by their 
projections. 
Warsh also announced that he is creating five task forces to investigate Fed 
communications, the Fed’s balance sheet, the Fed’s use of and reliance on existing data 
sources, productivity and jobs in “an era of transformation,” and the Fed’s inflation 
frameworks.  The task forces will include Fed staff economists as well as people from 
outside the Fed, including non-economists. 
Warsh appeared particularly focused on reconsidering Fed communications and 
especially on making changes to the Summary of Economic Projections.  He also said 
that “press conferences are useful, but when you have one you want to make sure you 
have something important to say,” which could hint at a break from the current pattern 
of press conferences after each of the eight FOMC meetings. 
Fed officials have already discussed some of the topics assigned to the task forces

全球金融市場持續受地緣政治不確定性干擾 。然而，
整體外匯市場波動度在3月達高峰後持續下降，成熟
市場外匯波動度指數創 2022年1月以來新低 。觀察
經濟基本面 ，各國經濟成長穩定但結構分歧 。製造
業因戰爭不確定性而加快回補存貨 ，全球製造業活
動升溫。另一方面 ，油價飆升推動通膨則衝擊服務

《油價下行空間形成限制，能源通膨推動央行升息》
全球地緣政治風險持續壓抑能源供給 ，但近期美伊
衝突暫時降溫 ，油價自高點回落 ，但年初以來國際
油價漲幅仍高達6成。儘管近期軍事緊張情勢緩解，
美伊再度進入談判階段 ，然而荷姆茲海峽運輸仍未
恢復，對油價下行空間形成限制 。為了因應油價所
帶動的通膨預期，今年以來澳洲央行連續3次升息，
目前基準利率已達 4.35%，澳洲已成為成熟國家中
利率最高的國家 。緊縮政策帶動澳幣資金行情 ，澳
幣兌美元年初以來升幅達 7.6%。各國央行為因應通
膨壓力，貨幣政策將逐步由觀望轉向升息 ，並對非
美貨幣形成正向支持。
另一方面，美國聯準會認為 2026年通膨雖持續降溫
但具僵固性 。目前評估長期實質利率加上通膨目標

(中性利率 )落在3%-4%區間，而現行聯準會基準利
率為3.5-3.75%，確實已經逼近中性利率區間上緣，
代表目前聯準會貨幣政策為中性偏緊。展望後續，6
月18日聯準會利率會議將由新任主席華許主導 ，而
華許已明確表態將調整聯準會政策方針 ，主要圍繞
在減少資產負債表的應用，簡化並減少對外前瞻指
重點摘要
• 澳洲連續3次升息
• 聯準會貨幣政策為中性偏緊

引，建立新的通膨評估指標 ，預期對美元影響有限 。
本行認為 ，美國貨幣政策仍處於降息循環 ，降息時
間因地緣政治而遞延但不代表已結束 ，預計基準利
率往中性利率下緣3%靠攏。因此，非美央行逐步往
緊縮政策靠攏 ，利差效應將帶動資金回流非美國家 ，
美元下半年維持弱勢盤整格局。
《非美貨幣走勢分化加劇，建議增持歐元部位》
非美貨幣近期表現分歧 ，其中英鎊與日圓相對承壓 ，
而歐元展望則相對正向。
英國5月地方選舉執政黨大敗，若進行國會改選，英
國可能出現打破百年兩大黨格局的 「五黨分裂 」與
懸峙國會局面 。在沒有任何單一政黨能取得過半席
次的情境下 ，未來政策推動難度增加 ，削弱英鎊投
資吸引力。
日圓方面 ，4月外資淨買進證券金額創 20年以來新

高。即便資金大幅流入日本市場 ，但在財政擴張政
策與持續刺激措施影響下 ，市場對債務負擔的疑慮
升高，壓抑日圓表現 。高市政府宣布 7月推出3兆日
圓補充預算，並且將食品飲料消費稅由8%實質下調
至1%。加上17大戰略領域投資計畫日本政府已表態
「不設預算上限 」，加深市場對債務可持續性的疑
慮，預期後續刺激措施將持續壓抑日圓表現。
歐元方面 ，隨著中東地緣政治風險推升通膨預期 ，
市場對6月升息的預期升溫。儘管服務業活動轉弱，
但製造業活動因庫存回補而回升 ，加上消費者信心
出現止穩跡象 ，支撐整體經濟基調 。歐洲央行態度
轉趨鷹派 ，官員亦表態支持升息 ，本行預期歐元區
今年升息1-2碼，利差優勢有助歐元走強。整體而言，
資金偏好具利差優勢與政策支撐的貨幣 ，歐元相對
具備上行潛力 ，預估歐元將呈現緩步走升趨勢 ，後
續表現優於其他非美貨幣。

《英鎊投資關鍵-英國內部政治風險升溫》
英國5月地區選舉極右派改革黨再度取得更多議會控
制權。根據近期的5月地方選舉結果與全國民調趨勢
推估，若舉行國會改選 ，將打破過去百年的兩大黨
壟斷，形成史無前例的 「五黨分裂 」在此推估下 ，
沒有任何單一政黨能拿下過半席次 ，國會形成懸峙
局面。政治不確定性大幅削弱英鎊的投資誘因 ，加
上基本面內需消費疲弱，消費信心降到3年低點並且
連續3個月下滑。
總體經濟方面，英國私部門5月產出自2025年4月以
來首度下滑 ，主因服務業走弱 ，抵銷製造業擴張 。
需求疲弱與中東衝突衝擊信心 ，企業訂單及投資意
願保守。企業面臨產出下滑 、通膨壓力與就業縮減 ，

未來景氣與物價上行恐使 BOE陷入升息與衰退風險
兩難。
展望後續 ，英國政治不確定性風險恐將造成政策面
停滯，短線關注 6/18梅克菲爾德選舉 。同時，英國
經濟面再度面臨下行風險，BOE維持現狀的可能性加
大。5月將英鎊評等調整至中性，預計英鎊兌美元呈
現區間整理格局。

瑞士PMI
《瑞郎投資關鍵-瑞士總體經濟面有所改善，瑞郎表
現持穩》
瑞士央行前次會議紀錄顯示，4Q25GDP成長0.2%，
產出動能主要由製藥業回升所帶動，服務業持續擴張
(服務業PMI一度創3年新高)，就業市場穩定。內部景
氣方面，瑞士4月PMI持續上升，製造業產出與積壓
訂單持續改善。服務業PMI小幅回落但仍處於高點。
瑞士企業普遍預期營收將溫和成長，但能源價格上升
帶動運輸成本增加，加上瑞郎升值對企業獲利形成壓
力。受中東地緣政治風險影響，瑞士央行認為未來數

季經濟不確定性顯著上升。短期經濟成長可能趨緩，
但中期有望改善，瑞士央行預估2026年GDP成長約
1%。能源價格上升將推升短期通膨，4月消費者物價
指數升至0.6%，重返18個月以來高點。央行認為伊
朗美國戰事升級可能帶動能源價格並抑制成長，同時
推升瑞郎避險需求。展望後續，利率期貨市場預期瑞
士央行6/18利率會議維持政策利率0%不變。在消費
者物價指數脫離通縮風險後，央行干預強勢瑞郎的可
能性相對降低。同時觀察歐元區及英國短線貨幣政策
傾向鷹派，利差擴大對瑞郎不利，預期瑞郎區間盤整，
3Q26美元兌瑞郎目標價0.82。

重點摘要
• 外資買盤並未帶動日圓走強
• 7月制定補充預算 3兆日圓計
劃
• 政府表態日本成長戰略無預
算上限
《日圓投資關鍵-補充預算令日圓回測 160關卡，日
本財政刺激壓抑日圓表現》
4月日本外人投資淨買進證券金額達9.4兆日圓，創下
2005年統計數據以來單月歷史新高 。然而，強勁的
外資買盤並未帶動日圓走強 。4月USDJPY價格均在
158以上盤整，直到4/30政府干預匯市帶動匯價回落
至156，但技術面形成多日下影線支撐 。進入5月，
USDJPY再度向上挑戰160關卡。日本首相高市宣布
於7月制定補充預算3兆日圓計劃。其中包含汽油補
貼、電力和天然氣補貼、支持企業融資以及補充儲備
金。除此之外，7月同步將兌現選舉時期所承諾的消

費稅減免，自民黨於2月眾議院選舉中承諾將食品飲
料消費稅從目前的8%降至0%(技術性將降至1%)。
2H26高市政府將繼續面臨許多財政事件。除了上述
補充預算以及消費稅減免外，2025年11月高市成立
日本成長戰略本部也對財政前景帶來隱憂。日本成長
戰略劃定17大戰略領域，政府已表態無預算上限 。
研擬透過追加預算對半導體、AI、安全保障與能源轉
型給予直接補貼，並且支持由國家主導關鍵產業重組，
加速關鍵企業的併購與整併 。目前17個戰略領域投
資措施仍在討論階段。公共投資已及私人投資規模尚
未公布，但可預期日本政府進一步擴大相關支出，成
為日債及日圓的主要利空因素。
整體而言，財政支出大幅激勵日本股市續揚，但持續
攀升的公債殖利率以及弱勢日圓凸顯投資人對於債務
展望不確定性的擔憂。同時，國會改選過後自民黨一
黨獨大，意味擴大支出將不會受到挑戰。且自民黨民
調仍居高不下，而高適早苗的支持率仍高達68%，顯
示民眾對於施政方針的支持 。除此之外，5/21自民
黨成立「國力研究會」，入會人數達347人，自民黨

籍國會議員約8成入會，凸顯高市已鞏固黨內的支持，
日本財政擴張政策短期難有阻力。因此可預期後續財
政刺激計劃繼續壓抑日圓表現。

◼ 盤勢結論： 本次拋售主力集中在今年漲幅極高
的AI客製化晶片及記憶體 ，台韓股市同樣受挫 ，
顯示市場在估值偏高下對負面訊息的耐受度低 ，
不過恐慌情緒並未蔓延至其他類股 ，6/5標普
500成分股中仍有逾半上漲。
◼ 投資建議： 利率上揚帶動資金去槓桿化 ，部分
個股估值過熱 ，將放大短線相關板塊的波動性 ，
現階段應優先重視風險和槓桿管理 ，留意資產
配置再平衡 ，待槓桿有所下降 ，將提供AI核心
受惠股更具吸引力的中長期布局機會。
熱門議題/資產影響推估 研究主軸與觀點
➢ AI先前漲勢驚人 ，短線負面消息帶動獲利了結：
博通財報、輝達在Vera CPU搭配的記憶體容量可
能減少等產業訊息先行影響盤勢 ，美國優於預期
的就業數據帶動降息遞延的預期 ，美債利率上揚
成為股市大幅修正的觸發點。
➢ AI週期並非終結 ，貨幣政策轉向升息機率低 ：企
業獲利仍在上修 ，而非單純靠估值膨脹 ，聯準會

降息遞延尚未轉向升息 ，本次市場震盪反映資金
與情緒重新平衡的過程，而非AI產業週期的終結
股市震盪修正理由
• 盤勢回顧-跌勢集中AI相關 P3

美國半導體與台韓科技股劇烈修正，雙率上揚但未失控
AI客製晶片與記憶體跌幅最重，防禦性類股穩健
盤勢回顧
美元和美債利率向上但未破新高，信用利差穩定
3
• 半導體類股漲多獲利了結，利匯率上揚但未失控：6/5拋售主力集中在今年漲幅極高的AI客製化晶片及記憶體，
台韓股市同樣受挫，顯示市場在估值偏高下對負面訊息的耐受度低，不過恐慌情緒並未蔓延至其他類股，當日標
普500成分股中仍有逾半上漲，防禦性類股如核心消費、公用事業、健護均表現穩健。然而。就業數據佳帶動美
國10年期公債殖利率來到4.5%之上，美元指數突破100，但都未破今年以來高點，信用利差則表現穩定。
• 短期政經事件干擾存，留意資產平衡配置：美伊情勢未明但油價並未失控上揚，加上AI需求推動企業獲利成長，
本行對於盤勢發展並未轉趨悲觀，然提醒短線過熱與政經干擾風險，變數包含: 1). 原油庫存至6月底將進入壓力區，
海峽運力是否能逐步回升、2). 新任聯準會主席是否產生新變數。則於股市急漲且廣度下降，短線過熱、動能反

轉的風險提高，需留意未來1-2個月盤勢來回震盪的風險。

科技股反映估值與需求雜音，但AI長期趨勢不變
美國雲端巨頭資本支出將延續到2027年
事件分析
台股融資餘額已接近歷史高點
4
• 科技股重挫主因為為「估值回歸」與「需求雜音」：6/5科技股重挫主因為為「估值回歸」與「需求雜音」。本
次半導體拋售集中在今年漲幅超過200%的AI客製晶片及記憶體，例如邁威爾(MRVL)、美光(MU)等。博通財報低
於預期與輝達記憶體配置調整造成投資人對AI晶片類股獲利了結。雖然資金槓桿風險偏高、部分個股估值過熱，
以及市場對供應鏈負面訊息高敏感下，對科技股造成劇烈波動。但機構預測2027年美國雲端巨頭資本支出仍將成
長50%，代表AI硬體未來一年業績成長動能仍是很強勁。
• 台股急跌非崩盤，AI成長動能仍在：台股受到評價過熱加上散戶融資 、期貨及槓桿ETF部位偏高，以及近期積
極搶進主動式ETF，使市場對利空訊息的敏感度顯著提高 。本次市場震盪反映資金與情緒重新平衡的過程 ，而
非AI產業週期的終結，現階段投資人應優先重視風險控管與槓桿管理，避免在市場情緒極度恐慌及悲觀時追殺

或過早攤平，待融資餘額明顯下降、恐慌情緒充分宣洩後，將提供AI核心受惠股更具吸引力的中長期布局機會。

### Page 5

©  CTBC
聯準會降息時間遞延，新任主席的框架將對市場帶來波動
就業穩定成長，聯準會降息預期遞延
貨幣政策
新任聯準會主席的全新框架將對市場帶來波動
5
• 就業表現強勁 ，研判聯準會重啟降息時間將遞延至 2027/3：美國5月非農就業新增17.2萬人，大幅高於市場預
估的8.5萬人，失業率持穩於4.3%，帶動市場對貨幣政策預期出現很大調整，利率期貨已反映今年可能升息的可
能性。本行仍認為下半年經濟將略為減速，但基於近期的就業成長趨勢，失業率上升的幅度將較原預估來的小，
因此本行將聯準會2026/12將重啟降息的預估調整至2027/3。然本行仍認為目前數據不足以支撐升息，主要因就
業數據分項中以政府、醫療等非景氣循環產業的就業最為穩定，薪資也沒有加速成長跡象。
• 新任主席若引入截然不同的框架，市場波動可能放大：雖市場重點關注聯準會是否能繼續降息，但新任主席華
許的框架改革可能是更大的重點。過去20年三任聯準會主席沿用同一套框架，即強調透明度的溝通方式、充裕
準備金架構，多次在市場波動之際成功降低衝擊。而華許強調不應過度溝通，將使市場共識較難凝聚。縮表立

場將使長天期利率缺乏來自聯準會的壓制力量，在美國財政壓力日益增加之際，高利率環境恐將維持更久。
資料來源：左，高盛，2026/6；右，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6。
美國非農就業新增人數(千人)
預估能維持失業率維持穩
定的非農就業人數門檻

©  CTBC
政策適應期易出現修正，縮表將加劇貨幣和公債市場的緊縮
聯準會主席上任的適應期，股市易出現修正
貨幣政策
資料來源：左， Barclays，Bloomberg，2026/6；右，Bloomberg，2026/6。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6
• 政策適應期易出現修正，縮表將加劇貨幣和公債市場的緊縮 ：歷史數據顯示，新任聯準會主席上任初期(前3到
6個月)，美股易出現修正，主要是因市場過去熟悉的貨幣政策節奏、決策風格以及對經濟數據的容忍度面臨重
新洗牌。而聯準會的對外溝通是一門精密的政治藝術，措辭不夠嚴謹或市場過度解讀，都容易導致市場出現重
新定價。不過這通常也僅是短期干擾，真正要留意的還是華許的縮表立場。就近年經驗，2013年柏南克的縮表
宣言造成公債殖利率急升，2018-19年造成回購市場危機，貨幣和公債市場的流動性緊縮最終都有傳導到股市 。

海峽持續低運力，6月底面臨營運壓力
荷姆茲海峽運力還未見恢復(至5/23)
中東地緣
資料來源：左，EIA，2026/6；右，Bloomberg，JPMorgan， 2026/6 ，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6
• 中東局勢反覆、海峽持續低流量，6月底庫存壓力浮現：即便中東再升溫為熱戰的風險不高，但美伊談判反覆，
使荷姆茲海峽遲遲未能恢復正常通行；據EIA預估，全球原油供需赤字將延續至第三季，期間布蘭特原油價格
亦持續維持在百元水準。基此，近月全球可見原油庫存持續快速下滑，在高油價與下游需求調整影響下，預估
6月供需赤字約為700萬桶/日；然若海峽運力限制延續至第三季，原油庫存恐降至接近營運壓力臨界點，供給
短缺將擴大至900萬桶/日，布油可能上行至120美元以上，進而對供給面產生負面制約，直接拖累實體經濟成
長動能。

• 市場廣度仍未打開，股市回檔風險加大：目前續創歷史新高的幾個主要市場，如美、台、韓、日，除了廣度與價
格開始背離，平均廣度目前也僅有5成(成分股>200日均線比例)，顯示資金集中度上升。此外，過往美股下跌超
過5%前，部份股票會提前轉弱。目前標普500有成分股有59%較200日高點下跌超過10%，比例為2023年多頭行
情啟動以來之最，其中下跌超過20%亦有29%，顯示高油價或地緣不確定的衝擊，持續在發酵，短線需留意政策
或地緣發展不如預期，導致漲高的股市出現連帶拉回。

### Page 9

©  CTBC
短期留意地緣與央行變數，中期仍偏正向看待
投資策略
• 景氣仍具延續力 ，貨幣政策調整非反轉訊號 ：隨著製造業景氣加速，通膨升溫，寬鬆貨幣政策進入調整期，但
歷史經驗顯示，政策觸頂並不意味景氣或資產表現立即反轉。從寬鬆政策高點至製造業PMI觸頂的過渡階段，多
數資產仍能維持正報酬，顯示政策調整初期景氣動能往往仍具延續性。在當前製造業庫存偏低的結構下，成本與
外部不確定性不易引發去庫存壓力，政策轉向更多是對既有環境的回應，而非景氣立即反轉的訊號。
• 短期留意地緣與央行變數 ，強化資產配置再平衡 ：然在當前資金高度集中與高槓桿的投資環境下，市場出現風
吹草動，就很可能進一步放大跌幅，形成多殺多的踩踏現象，現階段提醒資產配置再平衡，將風險平衡與槓桿管
理列為首要任務；各國貨幣政策短線轉向觀望恐使公債利率波動放大的情況再延續，然在景氣週期向好趨勢不變
下，美國及新興非投資級債、新興主要貨幣主權債與股市連動性高，受公債波動影響相對較低。中長期隨通膨回
升，庫存回補範圍預料將逐步擴大，原先看好具有結構成性成長題材(亞洲AI供應鏈、美國供應鏈重建）及政策支

持產業（國防、關鍵資源、藥品），其趨勢有望再度被強化。
9
資料來源：中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/6/8

市場投資展望
《谷歌增資加大押注 AI，持續看好指數上行》
谷歌宣布發行新股籌資達 847.5 億美元，為史上最
大科技股權融資 ，資金將用於 AI資料中心與算力建
設，支應顯著上升的資本支出 。私募與承銷價格的
折價與 2–3% 股權稀釋短期壓抑股價，但不改變谷
歌的長期論述 。儘管市場可能會更加關注現金流與
投報率壓力 ，但谷歌搜尋與雲端業務動能強勁 、AI
變現加速，基本面持續走強，市場在 26Q1 財報後
持續墊高對谷歌的獲利預期 。除此之外 ，本行預期
臉書在廣告量仍在成長之際將見到廣告單價成長加

素，加上付費 AI 工具、付費訂閱，將有助於臉書營
收增溫。整體而言 ，谷歌與臉書在廣告 、雲業務與
AI 驅動下，仍將支撐本行看多通訊服務指數。
• 通訊服務權值股谷歌發行增資，
以因應資本支出，加大押注 AI。
• 增資為谷歌股價帶來短期逆風，
但不影響谷歌的長期敘事。
• 市場在谷歌 26Q1 財報後持續
上調谷歌的獲利預期。
• 臉書廣告單價進入 26Q2 後優
於去年同期，同時廣告量仍在
成長且同步推出付費訂閱、付
費 AI 工具，有助營收成長動
能。
• 本行持續看好通訊服務指數在
谷歌與臉書下的表現。
1
資產 2Q26 看法 投資展望
通訊服務 買進 通訊服務主成分谷歌與臉書財報表現皆優於市場預期。雖然 AI 變現
的節奏使兩公司股價反應有別，但鑑於谷歌增溫的營運、臉書穩固的
護城河與世足賽、美國期中選舉將到來，本行持續對通訊服務 2Q26
表現正向看待。

2
《通訊服務權值股谷歌發行增資，股價短期逆風》
通訊服務主要成分股谷歌 (GOOGL) 母公司
Alphabet 在 6/1 宣布發行總值約 847.5 億美元的新
股，創下科技史上最大規模的股權籌資紀錄。籌資
結構分成三層。其一是波克夏海瑟威 (BRK.A/B，
Berkshire Hathaway) 私募認購約 100 億美元，分
別以每股 351.81 美元認購 50 億美元的 A 類股，以
及每股 348.2 美元認購 50 億美元的 C 類股。此舉
使其持股增加約 49% 至 8,640 萬股，佔流通股的
1.3%。其二是承銷公開發行約 350 億美元，以每股

約 355.2 美元發行 2,550 萬股 A 類股，及每股約
351.8 美元發行 2,550 萬股 C 類資本股。其中包括
150 億美元的強制性可轉換優先股 (Mandatory
Convertible Preferred Stock)。最後一層是市價發
行計畫籌資約 400 億美元：此「按市價發行（At-
the-market）」計畫預計於26Q3 啟動，Alphabet
將不定期在公開市場釋出 A 類與 C 類股票。
波克夏海瑟威與承銷公開發行的價格低於 6/1 市場
收盤價 376.37 美元各約 5.5-6.5% 不等。除此之外，
市場分析也預估本次股權融資將造成約 2-3% 的股
權稀釋。上述兩因素導致谷歌股價在新聞稿發出後
因此承壓。
《谷歌籌資以因應資本支出，加大押注 AI》
本次股權融資的資金將用於擴建資料中心及訓練新
一代 AI 模型所需的算力建設，以因應對 AI 服務超
乎預期的需求。Alphabet 預估 2026 年資本支出將
高達 1,800 億至 1,900 億美元，且 2027 年將持續

增長。有外資券商指出，機構投資人 (即買方預期)
在本筆股權融資前對谷歌 2027 年的資本支出預期
已抵 2,750 億美元，高於市場賣方預期的 2,450 億
美元約 12%。而在本筆融資後，買方與賣方預期合
理來說應會進一步推升，外資券商指出目前買方預
期相較 2026 年預期的資本支出年增已達 50%。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
